## Document 001: 1-9月财政收支数据解读：9月财政收入同比转为微降

Source PDF: all report pdf/1-9月财政收支数据解读：9月财政收入同比转为微降.pdf
Report source: 国新证券股份
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

市场研究部 2026年10月25日
事件
10 月 23 日，财政部发布，1-9 月，全国一般公共预算收入
172292亿元，同比增长 5.1%；全国一般公共预算支出 210199亿
元，同比增长 1%。
解读
一、1-9 月财政收入增速比 1-8 月小幅回落，9 月财政收入同
比转为微降。
1-9月，全国一般公共预算收入 172292亿元，同比增长 5.1%，
比 1-8月回落 0.6个百分点，比 2025年同期提高 4.6个百分点。
其中，9 月，全国一般公共预算收入 15659 亿元，同比下降
0.1%，比 8月下滑 4.8 个百分点，比 2025 年同期回落 2.7 个百分
点。
1-9月，全国税收收入同比增长 6.1%，比 1-8月回落 0.5个百
分点，比 2025年同期提高 5.4个百分点；非税收入同比增长 1.2%，
比 1-8月回落 0.3个百分点，比 2025年同期提高 1.6个百分点。
其中，9 月全国税收收入同比增长 0.2%，比 8 月回落 5.4 个
百分点，比 2025 年同期回落 8.5 个百分点；非税收入同比增长

5.7%，比 8 月提高 6.9 个百分点，比 2025 年同期提高 17.1 个百
分点。
分中央和地方看，1-9 月，中央一般公共预算收入同比增长
8.3%，比 1-8月回落 0.8个百分点；地方一般公共预算本级收入同
比增长 2.7%，比 1-8月回落 0.4个百分点。
9月，中央一般公共预算收入同比增长 0.2%，比 8月回落 9.1
个百分点；地方一般公共预算本级收入同比-0.34%，比 8 月回落
1.05个百分点。
二、9 月国内增值税、国内消费税同比增速比 8 月提高，企业
所得税、进口税收、个人所得税、证券交易印花税等税种增速比8
月回落，证券交易印花税同比转为下降。
1-9 月，国内增值税 55416 亿元，同比增长 6%，比 1-8 月提
高 0.1 个百分点。其中，9 月同比增长 7.3%， 比 8 月提高 3.3 个
百分点。
宏观研究
9月财政收入同比转为微降
——1-9月财政收支数据解读
市场表现截至 2026.8.31

分析师：郝大明

国内消费税 12402 亿元，同比下降 4.1%，降幅比 1-8 月扩大
0.5个百分点。 其中，9月同比下降 8.5%，降幅比 8月收窄 5.5个
百分点。
企业所得税 34832 亿元，同比增长 7.1%，比 1-8 月回落 0.2
个百分点。 其中，9 月同比增长 0.4%， 比 8 月下滑 12.0 个百分
点。
个人所得税 13464亿元，同比增长 14.1%，比 1-8月回落 0.4
个百分点。 其中，9 月同比增长 10.5%， 比 8 月回落 1.3 个百分
点。
进口货物增值税、消费税 15171 亿元，同比增长 12.7%，比
1-8月提高 0.1 个百分点。其中，9月同比增长 12.9%， 比 8月回
落 2.0个百分点。
出口货物退增值税、消费税 18087亿元，同比增长 6.3%，比
1-8月提高 1.8个百分点。 其中，9月同比增长 29.0%， 比 8月提
高 30.7个百分点。
1-9 月，印花税 4121 亿元，同比增长 31.1%，比 1-8 月回落
4.5个百分点。其中，9月同比下降 11.7%， 比 8月下滑 26.8个百

分点。
1-9月，证券交易印花税 2391亿元，同比增长 65.1%，比 1-8
月回落 16.9 个百分点。 其中，9月同比下降 11.5%， 比 8月下滑
29.4个百分点。
三、1-9 月全国财政支出小幅增长，比 1-8 月小幅回落，9 月
同比微增。
1-9月，全国一般公共预算支出 210199亿元，同比增长 1%，
比 1-8月回落 0.2个百分点。
分中央和地方看，中央一般公共预算本级支出 32820 亿元，
同比增长 5.8%，比 1-8 月回落 0.2 个百分点；地方一般公共预算
支出 177379亿元，同比增长 0.2%，比 1-8月回落 0.1个百分点。
1-9月，财政支出同比增长的项目是，卫生健康支出同比增长
7.7%，比 1-8月回落 1.5个百分点，社会保障支出同比增长 5.2%，
比 1-8月回落 1.3个百分点，债务付息支出同比增长 4.7%，比 1-8
月回落 0.7 个百分点，科学技术支出同比增长 3.0%，比 1-8 月提
高 0.8 个百分点，教育支出同比增长 1.1%，比 1-8 月提高 0.4 个

百分点。
其余支出项目 1-9 月均同比下降，其中，农林水支出同比下降
5.1%，节能环保支出同比下降 12.5%，文化旅游支出同比下降
6.0%，交通运输支出同比下降 4.1%，城乡社区事务支出同比下降
1.5%。

四、1-9 月政府性基金预算收入及国有土地使用权出让收入无
同比大幅下降，9月同比降幅比8 月小幅收窄。
1-9 月，全国政府性基金预算收入 25010 亿元，同比下降
18.6%，降幅比 1-8 月收窄 0.4 个百分点。分中央和地方看，中央
政府性基金预算收入 3682 亿元，同比增长 12.4%，比 1-8 月提高
0.2个百分点；地方政府性基金预算本级收入 21328亿元，同比下
降 22.3%，降幅比 1-8月收窄 0.6个百分点，其中，国有土地使用
权出让收入 16087亿元，同比下降 27.9%，降幅比 1-8月收窄 0.7
个百分点。
9月，全国政府性基金预算收入同比-15.9%，降幅比 8月扩大
12.1个百分点；分中央和地方看，中央政府性基金预算收入同比增
长 13.7%，比 8 月回落 40.6 个百分点，地方政府性基金预算本级
收入同比-18.4%，降幅比 8月扩大 8.1个百分点，其中，国有土地
使用权出让收入同比-23.2%，降幅比 8月扩大 10.6个百分点。

1-9 月，全国政府性基金预算支出 51948 亿元，同比下降
17.0%，降幅比 1-8 月扩大 0.6 个百分点。分中央和地方看，中央
政府性基金预算本级支出 3175亿元，同比下降 58.3%，降幅比 1-
8月收窄 3.5个百分点；地方政府性基金预算支出 48773亿元，同
比下降 11.3%，降幅比 1-8 月扩大 1.8 个百分点，其中，国有土地
使用权出让收入相关支出 21786 亿元，同比下降 18.5%，降幅比
1-8月扩大 1.1个百分点。
9月，全国政府性基金预算支出 62754亿元，同比下降 16.2%，
降幅比 8月收窄 5.2个百分点。分中央和地方看，中央政府性基金
预算本级支出 3692亿元，同比下降 54.4%，降幅比 8月扩大 45.6
个百分点；地方政府性基金预算支出 59062 亿元，同比下降
11.6%，降幅比 8 月扩大 10.6 个百分点，其中，国有土地使用权
出让收入相关支出 25565 亿元，同比下降 18.5%，降幅比 8 月收
窄 8.3个百分点。

五、预测10月财政收入同比增速小幅提高。
9月国内增值税、国内消费税同比增速比 8月提高，企业所得
税、进口税收、个人所得税、证券交易印花税等税种增速比 8月回
落，证券交易印花税同比转为下降。预测 10 月财政收入同比增速
小幅提高。
投资建议
1、9月国内增值税、国内消费税同比增速比 8月提高。
2、9月证券交易印花税同比转降。
3、9月出口退税同比大幅增加。
4、9月企业所得税、个人所得税同比增速回落。

## Document 062: 2026年7-9月期の実質GDP～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

Report source: NLI

実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移
民間最終消費支出 民間企業設備 その他民需 公需 外需
（資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」
( )内は年率換算値
(注）2602までは当研究所による改定見込値、2603は予測値
予測

１. 11/16に内閣府から公表される 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、前期比▲0.1％（前期比年
率▲0.3％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になったと推計される。 
２. 高水準の企業収益を背景に設備投資が前期比 1.4％と大幅に増加したが、天候不順の影響
などから民間消費が前期比▲0.3％と減少したこと、原油を中心に輸入が大幅に増加し、
外需が成長率を大きく押し下げたことなどから、小幅ながら 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成
長になったとみられる。 
３. 名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質GDPの伸
びを大きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％、前年比 2.5％と予測する。 
４. 2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時
的な影響が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回
復を妨げる可能性が高い。 
５. 現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むこ
となどから、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまる
と予想している。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-30

2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP 
～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸びを大
きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％（4-6月期：同 1.0％）、前年比 2.5％（4-6月
期：同 2.6％）と予測する。

なお、11/16に内閣府から 2026年 7-9月期の GDP速報が発表される際には、基礎統計の改定や季
節調整のかけ直しなどから、成長率が過去に遡って改定される。当研究所では、2026年 4-6月期の
実質 GDP成長率は、外需、設備投資が上方修正されることから、前期比年率 1.4％から同 2.3％へ
上方修正されると予想している。

2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。

1 10/30までに公表された経済指標をもとに予測している。今後公表される経済指標の結果によって予測値を修正する可
能性がある。

### Page 3

民間消費は前期比▲0.3％と予測する。 
実質賃金はプラスの伸びが続いているが、物価上昇率の高まりによって実質購買力が押し下げら
れていることに加え、台風や豪雨、長雨などの天候不順が個人消費を下押しした。 
2026年 7-9月期の消費関連指標を確認すると、延べ宿泊者数（うち日本人）は前期比 2.8％（4-6
月期：同▲0.9％）の増加となったが、自動車税の
「環境性能割」の廃止を受けて 4-6月期に前期比
10.7％の高い伸びとなった自動車販売台数が、その
反動で同▲7.5％と落ち込んだほか、外食産業売上
高が前期比▲0.3％（4-6月期：同 0.2％）、小売業
販売額指数が前期比▲2.1％（4-6月期：同 0.6％）
と減少した（いずれもニッセイ基礎研究所による
季節調整値、外食産業売上高、小売業販売額指数
は消費者物価指数で実質化）。

・住宅投資～弱めの動きが続く～

住宅投資は前期比▲0.2％と 2四半期連続の減
少を予測する。 
新設住宅着工戸数（季節調整済・年率換算値）
は、建築物省エネ法・建築基準法改正前の駆け
込み需要の反動で、2025年 1-3月期の 86.7万戸
から 4-6月期に 62.0万戸へ急減した後、7-9月
が 71.9万戸と持ち直し、その後は概ね 70万戸
台半ばで推移している。 
GDP統計の住宅投資は住宅価格上昇の影響も
あり弱めの動きとなっている。

・民間設備投資～高水準の企業収益を背景に増加～

民間設備投資は前期比 1.4％と 3四半期ぶりの増加を予測する。 
設備投資の一致指標である資本財総供給は 2026年 4-6月期の前期比 4.9％の後、7-9月期も同 4.9％
となった。また、機械投資の先行指標である機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は 2026年 4-6月
期に前期比 0.2％と 3四半期連続で増加した後、7、8月の平均は 4-6月期を 0.4％上回っている。 
日銀短観 2026年 9月調査では、2026年度の設備投資計画（含むソフトウェア・研究開発投資額、
除く土地投資額）が6月調査から 0.5％上方修正され、前年度比9.3％（全規模・全産業）となった。 
設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報

2026年度

・公的固定資本形成～2四半期連続の減少～

公的固定資本形成は、前期比▲2.4％と予測する。 
公共工事の先行指標である公共工事請負金額
は 2026年 7-9月期に前年比▲2.6％と 6四半期ぶ
りに減少した。また、公共工事の進捗を反映する
公共工事出来高（建設総合統計）は、2026年 4-6
月期に前年比 5.2％と 9四半期連続で増加した後、
2026年 7、8月の平均は同 2.4％と伸びが鈍化し
ている。 
2025年度補正予算による押し上げ効果が弱ま
っていることに加え、2026 年度当初予算の公共
事業関係費の伸びが抑えられていることから、弱
めの動きとなっている。

・外需～4四半期ぶりのマイナス寄与～

外需寄与度は前期比▲0.4％（前期比年率▲1.8％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナスを予測する。財貨・
サービスの輸出が前期比 2.0％、財貨・サービスの輸入が同 4.8％となるだろう。代替調達の進展に
伴う原油輸入量の急増を主因として 輸入が高い伸
びとなり、輸出の伸びを上回ったことから、外需は
成長率の押し下げ要因となった。 
2026年 7-9月期の地域別輸出数量指数を季節調整
値（当研究所による試算値）でみると、米国向けが
前期比 0.9％（4-6月期：同 2.8％） 、EU向けが前期
比▲2.4％（4-6月期：同 0.3％） 、アジア向けが前期
比▲0.3％（4-6 月期：同▲0.8％） 、うち中国向けが

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113) 金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

旺宏(2337)：下修 2027 年獲利展望 
建議：買進轉中立

一 、 旺宏為記憶體製造大廠 ， 主要產品為NOR 與 NAND 
Flash 
旺宏目前有一座 8 吋廠和一座 12 吋廠，主要產品分為
NAND Flash(包含 SLC NAND 以及 eMMC)、NOR Flash、
Mask ROM 與晶圓代工，NAND、NOR、ROM 與晶圓
代工分別佔 3Q26 營收比重 47%、46%、5%與 2%，為
全球最大 ROM 生產商以及 NOR Flash 領導廠商 ，2026
年資本支出 378 億元遠高於 18.61 億元。NOR FLASH
主要應用於消費性、PC、通訊、車用、工規、醫療和航
太市場 ， 旺宏於非PC 和消費性應用占比逐年增加 。ROM
主要應用於遊戲機市場，主要客戶為任天堂。晶圓代工
由 8 吋廠生產，主要產品為 MCU、IoT、電源管裡 IC 與
類比 IC 等產品。 
二、營收成長主要來自於產品漲價，3Q26 EPS 5.52 元
創新高但低於預期之 6.93 元 
3Q26 營收 242.37 億元(+27%QoQ)低於預期之 271.44
億元，主因為產能轉換影響產出較預期高，其中 NAND
和 NOR 營收分別+39%QoQ 和+21%QoQ， 受惠產品漲
價效益以及認列存貨跌價迴轉利益，毛利率由上季
64.4%提升至 70.5%，加計提列員工酬勞致使營業費用
高預期，營業利益 129.32 億元(+44%QoQ)低於預估值
20%，稅後純益 109.51 億元(+42%QoQ)，EPS 5.52 元
創新高但低於預期之 6.93 元。 
三、NOR 和 SLC NAND 價格漲勢延續 ， 估計4Q26 EPS 
6.73 元 
NOR 和 SLC NAND 價格漲勢延續，至於高價 MLC 迫
使客戶轉用 SLC 或 TLC，推估旺宏 eMMC 成長動能不
如前幾季強勁 ， 估計4Q26 營收272.8 億元(+13%QoQ)，
受惠於報價持續上漲，毛利率提升至 74.2%，在業外無
重大損益影響下，稅後淨利 133.63 億元(+22%QoQ)，
EPS 6.73 元。 
三、eMMC 現貨價回檔，將壓抑獲利想像空間 
3Q26 存貨 115.37 億元(+18%QoQ)， 主因為半成品增加，
至於 NOR 存貨評價自 3Q25 逐季貢獻利益，此增加毛
利率上檔空間。受惠於國際大廠三星停產低容量
4GB-32GB MLC NAND，致使旺宏低容量 eMMC 需求
大幅提升，由於此塊業務利潤最佳，故公司亦調配更多
產能於此，量價齊揚下致使NAND 今年營收成長性為各
產品線之最。3Q26 NAND 營收 QoQ+27%，儘管產能
調配持續增加出貨量，但 eMMC 占 NAND 營收 53%低
於上季之 58%， 推估主因在於價格漲勢不如SLC NAND，
至於 eMMC 新增產能效益需至 2H27 才能見到 ， 並以96
層 3D NAND 做為生產主力 。 根據CFM 閃存市場報價，
8GB-32GB eMMC 價格皆於 10 月轉趨疲弱，且集邦亦
認為高價 MLC 將促使客戶轉用 SLC 或 TLC，4Q26 MLC 
eMMC 價格恐將回檔 ， 在目前長約客戶營收占比極低下 ，
將壓抑旺宏明年獲利想像空間。 
就 NAND 3Q26 各應用營收表現而言，以電腦
+114%QoQ 成長性最佳，車用+19%QoQ 最差，消費性
電子、通訊、工規/醫療/航太則分別+43%QoQ、
+35%QoQ 和+41%QoQ。此外，集邦認為 MLC NAND
極度短缺將致使部分工控 、 車用客戶改採用SLC NAND，
並預期 2H26 SLC NAND 合約價較 1H26 大幅調漲
120-170%。 
四、NOR 產能資源將放在高容量 AI、太空以及醫療應
用上  
3Q26 NOR Flash 營收+21%QoQ，出貨量-12%QoQ，
主因並非需求減少，而是公司將產能分配至獲利更好的
NAND 和 eMMC 產品線，ASP 則受惠於產能排擠效應
而季增 37%，就 NOR 2Q26 各應用營收表現而言，除
車用營收+7%QoQ，電腦、消費性電子、通訊、工規/
醫療/航太皆有 QoQ 雙位數以上增幅。在NOR 2027 年
產業供需展望上，華邦透過併購 Infineon 記憶體事業取
得更多產能，旺宏將不再擴充擴充傳統應用 NOR，將資
源集中於高容量的 AI、 太空以及醫療應用上 ， 推估NOR
產業供需仍吃緊。

- 1 - 
個股報告

毅嘉(2402) 
光通產品高速增長，海外產能持續擴張 
投資評等 目標價 
逢低買進 $71 
前次投資建議：無

報告日期：2026.10.27 研 究 員：邱岳霖

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q4 光通訊量產，惟缺料與淡季影響營運動能，EPS 0.41元。 
➢ AI 光通訊放量帶動營收倍增，mSAP量產為毛利率改善關鍵，2027 年 EPS 3.1 元，考量公
司切入高速成長之光通賽道，未來幾年營運將高速增長，兆豐投顧給予目標價 71 元(23X 
27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 23.9%，投資評等為「逢低買進」 。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/10/22 收盤價： 57.30 ➢ 光通訊散熱型金屬外殼已於26Q3量產 ，FlipChip打件與
液冷測漏板於26Q4導入量產，預期光通訊營收佔比由
1%升至6%，然MLCC、DSP、PCB三項缺料影響，同
時進入消費性產品淡季，預估毅嘉 26Q4營收32.7億
元，QoQ -4.3%，毛利率14.5%，季增0.2ppt，稅後淨
利1.3億元，QoQ +23.9%，EPS 0.41元。 
➢ 展望後市，AI光通訊將成為毅嘉主要成長動能，預期AI
相關營收佔比將由26Q4之6%躍升至2027年40%， 係因
800G光收發模組需求持續強勁，目前主要客戶明年總
需求為27-30KK，此外1.6T/3.2T世代將於2028至2029
年接續拉升，帶動Flip Chip打件需求大幅擴大，而毅嘉
持續在馬來西亞擴產，雖帶動營收顯著提升，不過因採
turnkey出貨，料件佔BOM cost約80%至90%，故毛利
率僅約5%至8%，將稀釋整體毛利率。後續改善關鍵在
於mSAP高密度電路板，目前已啟動客戶樣品打樣，預
期27Q3量產，毛利率可達40%以上；此外散熱事業營
收倍增並轉虧為盈，攜手杰華精密切入光模組散熱外
殼，加上IMS延伸至儲能與機器人應用，將使產品組合
逐步轉佳。綜上所述，兆豐投顧預估毅嘉 2027年營收
248.9億元，YoY +94.9%，毛利率11.5%，年減2.5ppt，
稅後淨利9.5億元，YoY +159%，EPS 3.1元。考量公
司切入高速成長之光通賽道 ，未來幾年營運將高速增
長，兆豐投顧給予目標價71元(23X 27(F)PER) ，潛在
上漲空間23.9%，投資評等為「逢低買進」 。

個股報告 
資料來源：CMoney；兆豐投顧預估

營 運 分 析

毅嘉科技為軟板及機構整合廠  
毅嘉科技成立於 1983 年，初期以生產手機按鍵為主，於 2000 年掛牌上市
並跨足軟板製造領域，2020 年延伸至硬板打件並在跨入車用模組，2024 年
將戰略重心擴張至光通訊領域，從早期供應光收發模組用軟硬結合板，2026
年切入光收發模組 Flip Chip SMT，於 26Q3 完成認證並於 26Q4 放量生產，
預計 2027 年再導入 mSAP 高密度電路板，預期 27Q3 開始量產，此外毅嘉
於 26Q2 投資光通訊零組件廠商杰華精密(持股 46.25%)跨入光模組金屬外
殼。

毅嘉現主營軟板及機構整合業務：26Q3 應用別營收組成為車用 62%(智能座
艙 40%，連接板 19%，內飾件 3%)，其他消費 10%，遊戲 9%，穿戴設備
8%，其他產品 7%，手機 3%，光通訊 1%(26Q4 預估升至 6%)。生產基地
位於台灣林口廠(研發中心、散熱模組、IMS 研發中心)、大陸蘇州廠(車用軟
板、空板、 SMT、Mini LED)、大陸中山廠(穿戴裝置)、馬來西亞檳城及吉
打廠(25Q3 完工，產品為機構件、光通訊、車用、儲能 )，越南及匈牙利設
有銷售據點，北美據點評估中(德州)。

- 3 - 
個股報告 
26Q3 營收創今年新高，然新廠成本與匯損壓抑獲利，EPS 0.33元 
毅嘉(2402)公告 26Q3財報：營收 34.2 億元，QoQ +4.4%/YoY +18.4%，再
創近年新高，主要受惠車用及消費性電子產品需求成長帶動，毛利率 14.3%，
季增 0.1ppt，然因馬來西亞新廠初期營運成本與費用增加，營業利益較上季
衰退 10.5%，同時受人民幣升值產生匯兌損失，故稅後淨利 1 億元，QoQ 
-16.0%/YoY -45.1%，EPS 0.33元。

26Q4 光通訊量產，惟缺料與淡季影響營運動能，EPS 0.41元 
光通訊散熱型金屬外殼已於 26Q3 量產，FlipChip打件與液冷測漏板於 26Q4
導入量產，預期光通訊營收佔比由 1%升至 6%，然 MLCC、DSP、PCB 三
項缺料影響，同時進入消費性產品淡季，兆豐投顧預估毅嘉 26Q4 營收 32.7
億元，QoQ -4.3%/YoY +20.0%，毛利率 14.5%，季增 0.2ppt，年減 5.3ppt，
稅後淨利 1.3 億元，QoQ +23.9%/YoY -32.9%，EPS 0.41元。

AI 光通訊放量帶動營收倍增，mSAP量產為毛利率改善關鍵，2027年 EPS 
3.1元 
展望後市，AI 光通訊將成為毅嘉主要成長動能，預期 AI 相關營收佔比將由
26Q4之 6%躍升至 2027 年 40%，係因 800G 光收發模組需求持續強勁，目
前主要客戶明年總需求為 27-30KK，此外 1.6T/3.2T 世代將於 2028 至 2029
年接續拉升，帶動 Flip Chip 打件需求大幅擴大，而毅嘉持續在馬來西亞擴
產，Flip Chip 產線將由今年 3條提升至 2027 年 8-10條，2028 年進一步擴
充至 24條，雖帶動營收顯著提升，不過因採 turnkey 出貨，料件佔 BOM cost
約 80%至 90%，故毛利率僅約 5%至 8%，將稀釋整體毛利率。後續改善關
鍵在於 mSAP高密度電路板，目前已啟動客戶樣品打樣，預期 27Q3量產，
毛利率可達 40%以上；此外散熱事業營收倍增並轉虧為盈，攜手杰華精密切
入光模組散熱外殼，加上IMS 延伸至儲能與機器人應用，將使產品組合逐步
轉佳。綜上所述，兆豐投顧預估毅嘉2027 年營收 248.9 億元，YoY +94.9%，
毛利率 11.5%，年減2.5ppt，稅後淨利9.5 億元，YoY +159%，EPS 3.1 元。

評等分級：買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅超過+30%）; 逢低買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅介於+15%與+30%之間）; 區間操
作（預期未來六個月股價漲跌幅介於-15%與+15%之間）; 逢高賣出（預期未來六個月股價跌幅介於-15%與-30%之間）；賣出（預
期未來六個月股價跌幅超過-30%）。 
本刊所刊載之內容僅做為參考，惟已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量
投資之需求與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任。任何轉載或引用本報告內容必須經本公司同意。

Upward earnings revisions; reiterate our Buy on both names — We raise EMC’s
2026/27 /28E earnings by 20%/31%/44% to factor in price hikes and enhanced
product mix. We thus raise EMC’s TP to NT$7,400 (28x 2027E EPS) from NT$6,000
(30x 2027E EPS). For TUC, we raise 2026/27 /28E earnings by 21%/29%/27% to
factor in price hike and enhanced product mix as well. We lift our TUC TP to
NT$2,300 (23x 2027E EPS) from NT$1,950 (25x 2027E EPS). We lower PE multiple

for both names given recent AI concerns amid the rate hike backdrop.

### Page 12

Taiwan PCB & Laminates
29 October 2026 Citi Research
12
Elite Material
Company description
Established in 1992 in Taoyuan, Taiwan, EMC is a global leading copper clad
laminate (CCL) and prepreg supplier for HDI and smartphone applications.
EMC has developed several green materials products, from Mid Loss, Low
Loss, Very Low Loss, Ultra Low Loss down to Extreme Low Loss categories. In
the past few years, EMC has continued to gain presence in AI datacenter,

networking and HPC sectors. In the AI server market, EMC is one of the key
suppliers to global AI GPU customers and a major supplier to US CSPs for AI
ASIC projects.
Investment strategy
We are positive over EMC’s long-term growth outlook as we believe its
technology leadership in high-end CCL should position it well to benefit from
the structural spec migration in PCB materials amid the AI demand boom. We
expect EMC to maintain its market share in AI GPU projects and to gradually

gain share in next-gen ASIC projects in 2026. Given its leading position in
high-end CCL, sufficient raw material support, expanding capacity and solid
earnings outlook, we rate EMC a Buy.
Valuation
Our target price for EMC is set at NT$7,400, based on a target PE multiple of
28x on our 2027E EPS average. We believe our target PE multiple, at the peak
of its forward PE average in the past 5 years, is justified by a strong earnings
outlook supported by its leading position in CCL supply for AI GPU and ASIC

(high m/s) from 2026E onwards. We also believe the continued spec upgrade
for CCL, price hikes and product mix enhancement should help support its
margin expansion going forward. At our target price, the shares would trade at
55x/28x 2026E/27E PB.
Risks
Citi’s quant system rates EMC high-risk given high share price volatility, which
we largely attribute to frequent debates over the spec upgrade for next-gen AI
servers. In general, we believe some investors initially tend to have high hope

for aggressive spec upgrade without sufficiently considering the scalability
and cost structure of CCL products, which then leads to a shortfall in the end.
However, from a longer-term perspective, we see a decent and clear spec
upgrade trend with meaningful ASP growth potential among AI servers of
different generations. As such, we do not assign a high-risk rating.
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) a slower-than-expected CCL upgrade trend; 2) weaker-than-

expected AI server demand; 3) production bottlenecks in AI supply chain (eg,
glass, copper foil, foundry or OSAT); 4) unexpected share loss in key AI server
projects; and 5) stricter practice of OOC policy by end customers.
TUC

### Page 13

Taiwan PCB & Laminates
29 October 2026 Citi Research
13
Company description
TUC was established in 1974 and mainly produced optical glass initially. Since
1997, it started to provide copper clad laminate (CCL) and prepreg.
Headquartered in Hsinchu, Taiwan, TUC has an extensive global service
network spanning mainland China, Japan, Korea, the US and Germany. It has
been specializing in high-speed CCL for networking and now has a strong
position in supplying 800G switch opportunities. The company also started

to supply AI ASIC from 2025.
Investment strategy
We expect increasing AI ASIC exposure for TUC as it continues to see new
project opportunities among US CSP customers, including the high-end CCL
market for Trainium chip. We like TUC as it has production expertise in high-
speed CCL products, especially in networking, such as 800G, and has long
built solid relationships with upstream high-end materials suppliers to ensure
raw material support. We rate TUC a Buy given meaningful 800G ramp from

2026E onwards and its increasing opportunities in AI projects within CSPs
and enterprise players.
Valuation
Our target price for TUC is set at NT$2,300, based on a target PE multiple of
23x on our 2027E EPS. We believe our target PE multiple, at the peak of TUC’s
PE average in the last upcycle, is justified by its strong margin expansion
potential on a higher sales mix from AI ASIC server/800G products and price
hikes on supply tightness. We believe TUC’s growth outlook is well supported

by AI ASIC and 800G with its capacity ramp in Thailand from 3Q26 onward.
At our target price, the shares would trade at 2026E/27E PB of 53x/23x.
Risks
Citi’s quant system rates TUC High Risk given high share price volatility, which
we largely attribute to frequent debates over the spec upgrade for next-gen AI
servers. In general, we believe some investors initially tend to have high hope
for aggressive spec upgrade without sufficiently considering the scalability

and cost structure of CCL products, which then leads to a shortfall in the end.
However, from a longer-term perspective, we see a decent and clear spec
upgrade trend with meaningful ASP growth potential among AI servers of
different generations. As such, we do not assign a High Risk rating.
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) a slower-than-expected CCL upgrade trend; 2) weaker-than-
expected AI server/800G demand; 3) production bottlenecks in AI supply

chain (e.g., glass, copper foil, foundry, or OSAT); 4) unexpected share loss in
key AI server projects; 5) flexible practice of OOC policy by end customers;
and 6) slow development of the PCB industry in Southeast Asia.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.
Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk

rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered

stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also

suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in

exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility

or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day

period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be
different from and does not affect a stock’s fundamental equity rating, which reflects a longer-term total absolute return

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

information. The information contained in this report may differ from information a private company may subsequently make
public or otherwise share with investors. Continuation of publishing private company reports or private company sections
within reports is at Citi's discretion, in accordance with internal policies.

months of the year), (2) new Kunshan capacity (total 1.2mn sheets/month or 21% of the
company’s current capacity) ramp up from December, and (3) the mass volume
shipment for several new AI projects (Vera Rubin + TPU v8 etc.) with a better demand

For TUC, with an even faster product mix improvement speed (due to the lower base vs.

EMC) and the solid pricing hike uptrend for both high end and mid end CCL (15-20%
QoQ average pricing hike in 4Q), we expect the company to deliver a revenue growth
rate at 18% QoQ, and believe the GM will reach 40.8% (+3.9ppt QoQ) in 4Q26,
despite the slowing demand from key AI ASIC customer due to the transition period of
the ASIC projects whose capacity will be replaced by other high-end Ethernet switch
projects (also M8.5/M9 grade CCL).
Key update - EMC now plan to expand capacity to 15.25mn sheets/month from

5.85mn sheets/month capacity today, with solid demand
EMC’s BOD has approved additional capacity expansion plans extending into
2029/30E. EMC now targets year-end capacity of 8.70/10.45/11.95/15.25mn
sheets/month in 2027/28/29/30E (implying 27% 2026-30E CAGR), which is the first
time EMC announced its new capacity expansion plan after 1H28. EMC mentioned that
capacity expansion is supported by LTAs with customers who secure volumes (EMC does
not proactively initiate LTA negotiations). EMC indicated that despite its proactive

capacity expansion planned for 2027-30E, it would still be insufficient to meet with
customer demand secured under signed LTAs (demand under LTA extends at least 3Y
ahead).
EMC’s near-term capacity expansion remains intact, as the capacity expansion in
Kunshan (with additional 1.2mn sheets/month, 21% of current capacity) could
come online as early as Dec 2026 and continue through May 2027 (with new capacity
coming online every month), the expansion was originally scheduled in 2 phases, with

600k sheets/month each coming online in 1Q27 and 3Q27. The accelerated ramp-up
will offset the rescheduling of Guanyin capacity expansion, where the 300k sheet/month
expansions originally scheduled for 4Q26 have been deferred to 2H27, mainly due to
shortages of construction capacity in Taiwan as well as long equipment lead time +
Taiwan government’s strict policy on labor safety related requirement (however, we only
see delay in Taiwan, while all other plans are basically in-line or earlier than the

company’s original expectation).
Investment view - Solid margin beat + expanding margin, suggesting a stock
performance outlook; buy in the dip for both EMC & TUC
We notice that the BBG consensus for 2027 EMC/TUC GM is only at 38%/34%, and we
see upside risk to consensus EPS estimates for both EMC/TUC as the GM in 4Q could
both surpass 40-42%, without any possibility to see the GM to decrease in coming
quarters as the high-end CCL supply demand gap to further expand in coming years (see

here). As a result, we believe consensus 2027 GM could lift from 34-38% to 40-42%+ for
both companies in coming days, suggesting 10-25% EPS upward revision opportunity,
and recommend taking advantage to buy in this valuation dip (EMC/TUC only trades at
15x/12x of our bullish 2027 EPS estimate, which was far below the past 3 year range of

Moreover, EMC has mentioned all of its new capacity in coming years (capacity will

2H27, which is only for single customers’ AI GPU projects, but still implies a CCL 
migration trend in-line with our bullish view. For M9, we only see HGX R200 UBB and 
VR200 LPU adopt this solution in 2026, but see multiple new projects in 2027 will adopt 
this high ASP project (including several 1.6T ethernet switch projects, and Trainium 4/ 
TPU v9 could also see at least 50% adoption rate of the M9 CCL; moreover, the VR300 
rack should have 50%+ of its CCL content related to M9 grade CCL). We believe this new 
technology migration trend going into 2027 will further drive both companies’ ASP and 
profit in coming quarters, suggesting an even more favorable outlook for EMC/TUC’s EPS 
growth in coming years.  
Overall, we maintain our bullish view on high-end CCL players’ business outlook and 
margin expansion trend, and expect investors to start focusing on this solid margin 
expansion trend after this clean beat on GM/OPM in 3Q26; maintain Buy on 
EMC/TUC, with a new TP of NT$11,700/NT$3,460 (from NT$10,200/NT$3,010).

3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and 
expect EMC to increase its share from <10% in the Purley generation to 15-20%/20+% 
in the Whitley/Eagle Stream platforms. EMC is also proactively expanding its share in the 
switch market (40%/30%+ in 2024/23 vs. <5% before 2019), with an expanding AI 
customer base (only one 400G customer in 3Q20, to being one of the largest suppliers in 
2023), which should continue to benefit the company’s top/bottom line growth over 
time. We believe EMC’s leading position in the AI server CCL market should drive strong 
revenue growth and GM/OPM expansion in the long term, given that it is the major 
supplier for all Nvidia/Google/AWS AI server projects, and we expect it to maintain its 
leadership position. Considering its leading position in the high-end CCL industry, solid 
production skills and earnings growth outlook, we are Buy rated on the stock. With the 
shares trading at a 2026E P/E below AI server component suppliers and supported by a 
solid growth outlook, we view valuation as attractive. 
Key downside risks include: (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) 
rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and 
(3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Elite Material  
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$11,700 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).  
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising 
trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3) 
rising competition from mainland China peers. 
Investment Thesis - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
TUC is a key high-end global CCL supplier that focuses on M7+ grade high-speed CCL 
(key applications include high-end switches (100G+) and AI server materials), with 20%+ 
market share in the past two years. The company continues to expect its unit market 
share in the server industry to increase from 15% in Whitley and Purley processors to 
higher in the Eagle Stream generation, and plans to launch two types of products for 
low-end and high-end customers (T2A and T2C) to gain market share. Also, strong 
growth in 400G/800G switch shipments and new 800G switch shipments, scheduled for 
launch in 2025-27, should continue to be a key demand driver for TUC, in our view. 
Additionally, the company is working on high-end AI projects with CSP and enterprise 
players, which we believe should drive revenue momentum in the long term. We view the 
stock as undervalued vs. Taiwan CCL peers on a P/E basis and rate it Buy.  
Key downside risks include: (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL 
segment; (2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch 
shipments globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$3,460 is based on a 22x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(+2x STDV higher than the past 3-year industry average P/E multiple).

We hosted EMC’s 3Q26 post result call on October 29th. Key 
takeaways include: 
(1) EMC mentioned its proactive capacity expansion plans in coming 
years (capacity will go up to 15.25mn sheets/month by end of 2030 
vs. 5.85mn sheets/month now) are still not enough to support all the 
LTA they signed for the next 4 years, implying a solid demand 
outlook without much risk on oversupply.  
(2) In terms of the CCL pricing, the company mentioned it’s doing 
the second round of the high-end CCL (M7+) pricing hike this year, 
with 5-20% pricing hike ratio, which not only on M7/M8, but also on 
M8+/M9 grade CCL.  
(3) For 4Q26, EMC mentioned the conservative estimate on revenue 
growth rate should be at least 15-20% QoQ, with GM could at least 
expand to 38.0%-39.5% (note that the company estimated 3Q rev 
could grow 15% QoQ and GM could be 33.5%-36.5% back to 2Q26 
result call - here, but the actual outcome was revenue up 29% QoQ 
and GM hit 37.7%).  
(4) For substrate material, the company mentioned it is focusing on 
the high-end ABF substrate core-layer CCL (BT type, but for ABF 
substrate), and will hold 50% market share for the MTK TPU v8 ABF 
substrate. EMC also mentioned that it is now working with several 
customers on high end ABF used CCL qualification projects which 
the company expects to start shipment from 4Q27. 
(5) For the substrate material capacity, EMC now holds 300k 
sheets/month capacity, but will expand the capacity to at least 
1.2mn sheets/month by end of 2027. EMC mentioned the proactive 
expansion is their strategy on always prepare capacity ahead of 
customers’ demand, and mentioned these new capacity can also be 
used to produce high-end M9/M10/M11 CCL if the demand cannot 
fill all the capacity.

### Page 3

(6) In terms of the profitability of the substrate material, the company mentioned the
GM of this product will be at least 50%+, despite its relatively competitive pricing
compares to the market leader who has 65%+ GM on substrate material.
(7) EMC mentioned it does see AI customers start qualifying second or third sources to
improve the stability of the supply chain, which should give more room for competitors
to enter the market. However, the company continues to target 50% AI CCL market

share in the long term, and believes its best in class product quality and deep
cooperation with PCB/CSP customers can see it continue to hold the leadership position
in this high-end industry.
Investment view - read across for high-end CCL players
We maintain our positive view on the high-end CCL players, including EMC and TUC,
while we see both companies are enjoying not only favorable pricing conditions on
high-end CCL (due to the wide supply demand gap) but also the accelerating product

mix improvement speed (we expect M9 massive adoption to start from 2H27 with 50%+
adoption rate for next generation AI server, and expect EMC to start M10 CCL product
shipment from 2H27 with mass volume shipment to start from 2028). See our Oct 28
report.
Both EMC and TUC mentioned the proactive capacity expansion they are planning in the
next 3-5 years (both companies will have 150-250% more capacity in 2029-2030 vs.
today) are still not enough to support the high-end AI/LEO/switching customers’

demand, suggesting an even more favorable pricing outlook. We expect the high-end
CCL pricing will go up by 40%+ YoY in 2027 considering the increasing tightness
condition of the high-end CCL industry (vs. 20-30% YoY 2026), which should drive both
company’s GM further in coming years.
In terms of competition, while we already model EMC’s AI CCL market share to decease
from 60-70% in 2026 to ~50% in the next three years, we do believe EMC’s earnings will

grow by 90% 2026-29 CAGR even with this 50% market share. For TUC, we expect it
should be the key share gainer from EMC considering CSPs duo source policy (we think
TUC would be a strong option due to not only its better technology skillset but also its
proactive expansion plans while other high-end CCL players are not expanding capacity
or only expanding slightly on the capacity), and we expect the company will have
multiple AI customers including AI ASIC/GPU end customers from 2H27.

Overall, we are even more positive on EMC/TUC compared to 3 months ago, after seeing
customers solid plans on high-end CCL LTA and customers’ design upgrade on CCL spec
(we had expected M10 MP would happen in 2029 or beyond only). We believe
EMC/TUC’s key competition advantage is not only from the leadership position in the
high-end CCL market, but also their proactive capacity expansion plans which makes
them attractive to AI customers as most of the other high-end CCL players are not

expanding enough capacity in 2027/28.
Maintain Buy on EMC / TUC with TP of NT$11,700 / NT$3,460, implying 134%/150%
upside vs. last close.

### Page 5

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Elite Material  
EMC, a key supplier of high-end HDI material (70%+ market share) and SLP material 
(90%+ market share), has been focusing on the high-speed switch/server CCL market for 
3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and 
expect EMC to increase its share from <10% in the Purley generation to 15-20%/20+% 
in the Whitley/Eagle Stream platforms. EMC is also proactively expanding its share in the 
switch market (40%/30%+ in 2024/23 vs. <5% before 2019), with an expanding AI 
customer base (only one 400G customer in 3Q20, to being one of the largest suppliers in 
2023), which should continue to benefit the company’s top/bottom line growth over 
time. We believe EMC’s leading position in the AI server CCL market should drive strong 
revenue growth and GM/OPM expansion in the long term, given that it is the major 
supplier for all Nvidia/Google/AWS AI server projects, and we expect it to maintain its 
leadership position. Considering its leading position in the high-end CCL industry, solid 
production skills and earnings growth outlook, we are Buy rated on the stock. With the 
shares trading at a 2026E P/E below AI server component suppliers and supported by a 
solid growth outlook, we view valuation as attractive. 
Key downside risks include: (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) 
rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and 
(3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Elite Material  
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$11,700 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).  
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising 
trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3) 
rising competition from mainland China peers. 
Investment Thesis - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
TUC is a key high-end global CCL supplier that focuses on M7+ grade high-speed CCL 
(key applications include high-end switches (100G+) and AI server materials), with 20%+ 
market share in the past two years. The company continues to expect its unit market 
share in the server industry to increase from 15% in Whitley and Purley processors to 
higher in the Eagle Stream generation, and plans to launch two types of products for 
low-end and high-end customers (T2A and T2C) to gain market share. Also, strong 
growth in 400G/800G switch shipments and new 800G switch shipments, scheduled for 
launch in 2025-27, should continue to be a key demand driver for TUC, in our view. 
Additionally, the company is working on high-end AI projects with CSP and enterprise 
players, which we believe should drive revenue momentum in the long term. We view the 
stock as undervalued vs. Taiwan CCL peers on a P/E basis and rate it Buy.  
Key downside risks include: (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL 
segment; (2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch 
shipments globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

營收走勢圖

研究員/吳大任 TERRY.WU@uni-psg.com

研究部對創意的投資建議維持強力買進，係因:(1)3Q26 營收、毛利
率、營業利益率皆優於預期，(2)4Q26 雖因為 ECB 將送件未給財測，
但客戶對 CPU 需求來看仍持續上修 ，(3)北美車用晶片有望在 2Q27 初
導入 ， 中國車用客戶晶片市佔率持續提升 ， 且持續拓展海外客戶 ，27-
28 年貢獻幅度大幅跳升 ，(4)晶片大廠持續上修 CPU 與加速器的配適
性，更有效率的使用 GPU 之效能，(5)不僅 200 系列晶片，CSP 客戶
的 300 系列晶片創意也有所著墨。綜合以上，上修創意 26 年 EPS 預
估為 63.3 元(原估 54.76 元，+125%YOY)，同步上修 27 年 EPS 預估
為 245.9 元(原估 214.5 元，+288.5%YOY)， 及上修28 年EPS 為 467.7
元(原估 392.6 元，+95.9%YOY，以股本膨脹 3%計算)。考量 27 年下
半年創意的成長動能更加明確上修 ， 且費用控管十分優異 ， 客戶從雲
端 AI 延伸至終端 AI，CPU、加速器、自駕晶片及 CPO 皆有著墨，故
對創意看法更加樂觀，故先前 8600 元目前價到價後，仍維持強力買
進投資建議，目標價調升至 9850 元(27 年 EPS*40XPER OR 28 年
EPS*21XPER)。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營收大幅優於預期，OPM 更加優化：  
創意 3Q26 營收達到創新高且優於上修後的預期的 187.5 億
元，成長35%QOQ，成長117.7%YOY，其中量產營收達到166.7
億元，成長44%QOQ，推估來自於CPU 的貢獻較大，且在2H26
創意也有新增一些產能支援 ， 且NRE 也達到預估的區間上緣 ，
僅衰退 11%QOQ。 毛利率達到20.59%， 優於研究部預期的19%，
更優於市場預估的 19-20%，推估主要是出貨的晶圓良率更加
提升所致。費用控制非常良好，3Q26 費用僅 13.09 億元，低
於 2Q26 的 13.18 億元，但營收卻成長35%QOQ，故使得OPM 提
升至 13.6%，大幅優於先前預估的11%。稅後淨利達到創新高
的 22.73 億元(原估 16.02 億元)，成長 46.1%QOQ，成長
162.1%YOY，EPS 16.96 元(原估 11.96 元)。

2. 因 ECB 將送件，故無 4Q26 財測，但仍正向看待：  
因為創意的 ECB 即將送件，故此次財報後無法給出財測，但
研究部仍認為 4Q26 創意營運動能將上修，主要係因先前在上
半年所提到的投片保有量外，仍有多爭取的 WAFER，從創意
3Q26 每個月的量產營收就可以觀察到持續增加，單月量產營
收已經提升至 65 億元，預估 4Q26 將能持續穩定攀升。另外
NRE 方面，CSP 下一代 CPU 將預估在 4Q26 TAPE OUT，NRE 預

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

估將翻倍成長 。 綜合以上 ，因為量產營收持續優於預期， 故上
修 4Q26 營收為 244.2 億元(原估 236 億元)，成長30.2%QOQ，
成長 97%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為續創新高的 30.1 億元(原
估 25.35 億元)，成長32.5%QOQ，成長159.6%YOY，EPS 22.46
元(原估 18.92 元)。

3. CPU在Agentic AI世代重要性持續提升： 
根據INTEL的資料中心與人工智慧白皮書顯示，CPU 容量不足
可能造成編排卡頓、工具執行延遲、驗證循環變慢，導致GPU
在等待控制平面 （代理編排） 處理完成時利用率不足 。 若要將
代理型工作負載最佳化，就必須增加 CPU 數量，作為控制與
編排層，在兼顧 新式 AI 工作流程分散且多步驟本質的同
時，將資料高效提供給加速器。故根據INTEL實測顯示，在模
型大小為8B規模時 ， 建議加速器與CPU配比為1.4:1， 模型大小
擴大到70B時，建議配比為1:1。故在AMD簡報也顯示，2025-
2030年CPU產值規模將從260億美元提升至2030年的2200億美
元，CAGR達到50%。創意的CSP客戶所ASIC走到第八代後，CPU
與TPU的配置從先前1:4確實提升至1:2，並且全數從先前的
X86 BASE更改成自家研發的ARM BASE CPU。而CSP客戶第八代
產品放量時間點將落在4Q26末，2027年完整貢獻 ， 故從創意量
產營收看出 ， 因為配比提升CSP客戶對CPU需求持續增加 ， 故上
修CPU26年貢獻創意營收從12億美元上修至13-15億美元。在
27年方面，有鑑於客戶對於加速器及CPU的配飾性相對於第七
代提升一倍，加上下一代CPU將在4Q26 TAPE OUT，有望在2H27
開始貢獻。因為二代產品單價提升，故上修CPU貢獻營收程度
從35億美元上修35-40億美元。 
CSP二代CPU將有望在28年完整貢獻，ASP提升幅度較大，且三
代CPU目前已經開始著手研發 ， 故研究部認為 ，CPU對加速器的
配置開始往1:1走後，創意目前在客戶需求延續性強，且持續
有拿到次世代案件下，28年CPU貢獻幅度將達到50%YOY增長。

圖一、INTEL 資料中心白皮書內容

圖二、AMD 對 SERVER CPU 產值的預估

資料來源：AMD，統一研究部整理

車用將成為 27 年開始的新成長支柱 
(1)北美客戶： 
根據供應鏈訪查 ， 北美車用客戶晶片量產時間點將落在2Q26，
創意目前在 5 代晶片進度仍優於競爭對手，故以目前規劃的
投片量來看 ， 研究部維持先前預估貢獻約12 億美金營收不變 。
但根據北美客戶法說，該客戶希望將5 代晶片在 2027 年 4 月
導入機器人使用 ， 故研究部目前僅估算車用的部分 ， 尚未估計
機器人，若該客戶順利量產機器人，則貢獻創意營收有望上
修。 
(2)中國客戶： 
以中國客戶自行揭露的資料來看，全系列征程系列晶片出貨
套數已經高達 1000 萬套，在 26 年 9 月，該客戶的 CEO 在社
群媒體上表示，公司智駕晶片出貨量已達 1500 萬套。另外客
戶給出 26 年征程系列晶片將出貨 500 萬套，27 年將更達到
700 萬套的目標。先前預估 27 年以客戶出貨目標 700 萬套的
50%，350 萬套第 7 代征程晶片做估計，貢獻營收將達到 15-
20 億美元，此預估沒有變化，靜待客戶完成年報公告後再行
調整。

圖三、中國自駕車晶片公司 CEO 微博

資料來源：WEIBO，統一研究部整理

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

兩大車用客戶對創意來說 ，27年僅是開端 ， 首先北美客戶27年
才會導入5代晶片，且是機器人及無人計程車皆會導入，附帶
一提，先前展示的飛天跑車也是搭載5代晶片，故認為在客戶
量產機器人及無人計程車後，晶片需求在 28年將會超過翻倍
成長 。 另外在中國客戶方面， 除了本土市場外 ， 該公司積極拓
展海外市場，7代晶片LIFECYCLE有望比6代更久，28年目前也
以翻倍的狀況預估。

4. CSP客戶三代晶片仍有著墨機會： 
北美另一雲端客戶在10月初表示，200系列加速器已經實現量
產，該客戶已經在各地區DATA CENTER 部屬200系列晶片，而
200系列晶片後段為創意打造，推估LIFE CYCLE出貨量為數十
萬顆 ， 貢獻營收約3-5億美元 。 而在300系列方面 ， 先前市場傳
出主要供應商為美國晶片同業 ， 後來研究部訪查 ， 創意在27年
亦有BOOKING 2nm產品，而300系列晶片由N2P節點打造，故推
估創意亦有部分300系列晶片相關生意，故仍有營收貢獻。而
目前推估400系列晶片有望重回創意，挹注 28年後的營運動
能。

5. 26、27年上修預估，28年持續高度成長： 
創意3Q26營收已經大幅優於預期 ， 加上毛利率擊敗市場預期 ，
另外費用控管得宜 ， 使得3Q26 OPM不減反增 ， 另外4Q26雖然未
給財測，但在產能有所支持加上CPU需求持續推升下，營收也
略有上修，故上修創意26年營收預估為685.2億元(原估648.6
億元)，成長100.7%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為84.8億元(原估
73.28億元)， 成長125%YOY，EPS上修預估為63.3元(原估54.76
元)。 展望27年，CPU貢獻性持續上修 ， 另外也維持車用客戶的
預估值，另外本來就有CSP客戶的200系列加速器開始大幅量
產，300系列目前有所著墨，故上修27年營收預估為2458.5億
元(原估2328億元)，成長258.8%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為
329.5億元(原估287.5億元)， 成長288.5%YOY，EPS上修預估為
245.9元(原估214.5元)。最後，雖然目前28年投片等尚未確
定 ， 不過創意在CSP二代CPU、 中國及北美車用晶片 ， 接續世代
晶片皆有持續獲得新世代案件 ， 另外在CSP ASIC方面 ， 目前有
將從整合封裝能力開始切入 ，CSP 300/400系列晶片皆有斬獲 ，
故研究 部粗估28年創意營收將上修預估為 5145.8億元(原估
4627.5億元)，成長109.3%YOY，稅後淨利預估上修為645.5億
元(原估542億元)，成長95.9%YOY，EPS預估為467.7元(原估
392.6元，以膨脹後3%股本計算)。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

## Document 256: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK하이닉스 최대주주 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK하이닉스 최대주주 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
SK 하이닉스 최대주주 
SK 하이닉스 4 분기도 배당 주당 20,000 원 이상  
SK 하이닉스는 전일 주당 20,000원(실적반영: 10,000원, 키옥시아 특별배당: 
10,000원)의 3분기 배당을 발표하였다. 키옥시아 특별배당은 3분기 배당과 26년 회
계연도 결산배당으로 나누어 지급예정이다. 이에 SK스퀘어는 2조 9,220억원(1분기
와 2분기 배당수입은 각 548억원)의 배당수입을 수취하게 된다. SK하이닉스는 26년 
전체 현금 배당 규모는 30조원 이상을 발표했는데, 3분기까지 지급하는 배당금은 14.6
조원이기 때문에 4분기에도 최소한 주당 20.000원 이상의 배당이 예상된다. 이 경우 
SK 스퀘어의 26 년 배당수입은 6 조원에 달할 것으로 예상한다. SK 스퀘어는 ‘26년
~’28년 경상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 주주환원정책을 보유하고 있
어 SK스퀘어의 현 시가총액 대비 27년 주주환원율은 최소 1.5% 이상 될 전망이다.

SK 하이닉스 자사주 취득·소각으로 높아질 지배력 
SK하이닉스는 20조원의 자사주 매입을 발표하였다. 이 중 10조원은 임직원 보상목적
이며, 10조원은 주주환원 목적이다. 26년 총 60조원 이상의 자사주 취득·소각 예정(8
월 결의분 40 조원 포함)으로 임직원 보상 목적의 자사주 매입은 제외되기 때문에 26
년 실적 확정 후 추가적으로 10조원 이상의 자사주 취득이 예상된다. 기 취득한 자사주 
40조원(3.3%)는 11월 5일 소각 예정으로 소각 이후 SK스퀘어의 SK하이닉스 지분
율은 20.5%(2 분기말 기준)에서 21.2%로 상승하게 된다. 이번 발표한 10 조원
(1.0%) 자사주 소각시 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 21.4%이 되며, 추가로 10 조원을 추가 
소각한다면 21.7%까지 지분율이 상승하게 된다.

SK 하이닉스 대비 높은 베타 
SK스퀘어에 대한 투자의견 매수를 유지한다. SK스퀘어 NAV의 98%가량을 차지하
는 SK하이닉스의 현금배당 증가로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 재원이 확대되고, SK하이닉
스의 자사주 취득·소각으로 지배력이 강화되기 때문이다. SK하이닉스는 전일 컨퍼런스 
콜을 통해 29년까지 D램과 HBM 물량이 사실상 모두 솔드아웃된 상황이라 밝히며 주
가 반등 가능성이 높은데, SK 스퀘어 베타(1.8)는 SK 하이닉스(1.7) 대비 높기 때문에 
SK 하이닉스 주가 반등에 따른 SK 스퀘어의 동반 주가 상승을 기대할 수 있다. 현재 
SK스퀘어의 NAV 대비 할인율은 46.5%이며, SK하이닉스가 자사주60조원 소각시 
할인율은 49.4%까지 상승하게 된다.

2026-10-29

## Document 264: AI信仰迎来新考验，股价暴跌后Alphabet赴欧债市场融资

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI信仰迎来新考验，股价暴跌后Alphabet赴欧债市场融资.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
Alphabet 在三季度业绩不及预期、单⽇市值蒸发逾 5000 亿美元后，启动七档欧元债发⾏，
期限覆盖 2 ⾄ 19 年，以⽀持最⾼ 2050 亿美元年度资本开⽀。科技债需求⾛弱、信⽤利差⾛
阔之际，此次融资定价与认购情况将成为检验市场对 AI 巨额投资及回报前景信⼼的重要指
标。
摘要：
Alphabet 在第三季度业绩重挫、股价创纪录下跌后不⾜⼀周，即赴欧元债券市场启动新⼀轮融
资，直接检验投资者对⼈⼯智能巨额资本开⽀的信⼼是否依然稳固。
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠透露，Alphabet 周⼀开放了⼀项涵盖 2 年期⾄ 19 年期的七档欧元债券发⾏
簿记，承销⾏预计当天晚些时候完成定价。此次发⾏距 Alphabet 上周公布令市场失望的三季度
业绩仅数⽇，彼时公司单⽇市值蒸发逾 5000 亿美元，科技板块信⽤利差随之全⾯⾛阔。
此次融资将进⼀步充实 Alphabet ⾼达 2050 亿美元的年度资本开⽀计划，但其能否以合理成本完
成发⾏，将成为市场评估投资者对 AI 基础设施叙事信⼼的重要参照。与此同时，同期发⾏的科技

类债券普遍⾯临需求⾛弱、定价⾛阔的压⼒。
业绩重挫引发信⽤市场连锁反应
Alphabet 上周公布的三季度营收未达市场预期，外界随即对 AI 投资能否转化为实质回报的担忧骤
然升温，亦有声⾳认为 AI 正对其核⼼搜索业务构成潜在威胁。公司股价随之创下单⽇市值蒸发规
模历史纪录，达 5000 亿美元，并波及整个科技板块的信⽤利差。
信⽤违约互换市场的反应尤为显著。上周五， Alphabet 债务的违约保护成本单⽇跳升 6.4 个基
点，创有记录以来最⼤单⽇绝对涨幅，并触及约 91 个基点的历史⾼位。
据知情⼈⼠透露，本次欧元债发⾏共设七个档期，期限从 2 年延伸⾄ 19 年。其中 2 年期固定利率
档的初始定价参考利率为中间互换利率（ mid-swaps ）加约 60 个基点，⽽期限最⻓的 19 年期档
则报价于 mid-swaps 加 205 ⾄ 210 个基点区间。承销⾏为巴克莱、法国巴黎银⾏及德意志银⾏。
今年 5 ⽉， Alphabet 曾以六档结构在欧元债市场融资 90 亿欧元（约合 100 亿美元）。此番再度出

⼿，外界将密切关注最终定价与需求规模，以衡量此次业绩冲击对公司融资条件的实际影响。
科技巨头 AI 军备竞赛仍未放缓
尽管市场情绪趋于谨慎，科技巨头的资本开⽀承诺却未⻅任何收缩迹象。 Alphabet 维持了全年
最⾼ 2050 亿美元的资本开⽀指引，与 Meta Platforms 、微软、亚⻢逊及甲⻣⽂合计，上述五家
公司未来数年的⽀出承诺已超过 3 万亿美元。
科技公司通过债务市场为 AI 投资持续融资的趋势仍在延续。微软上周完成了 250 亿美元债券发⾏
—— 这是其⾃ 2017 年以来⾸次在美国投资级市场发⾏债券，该笔⼗档债券获得逾四倍超额认
购，彼时微软三季度业绩超出华尔街预期，市场情绪较为有利。
相⽐之下， Alphabet 此次发⾏⾯临的市场环境明显更为复杂。近期科技类债券发⾏普遍遭遇需
求⾛弱与定价⾛阔，此番融资能否顺利落地，将成为检验市场对 AI 叙事信⼼的⼀块重要试⾦⽯。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices
card-acceptance costs that had previously been surcharged
– and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-
plus’ approach to pricing. To the extent they can pass on
these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was

strong enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal
that demand is still outstripping supply.
• This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages
growth has been moderate for some years, though the
above-average increase in award wages this year will add
a little. Not all firms will seek to pass these cost increases
on, but some will, including because they have limited
scope to absorb the cost increase into margins.
• A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it

limits the imagination away from finding alternative,
efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with
recent academic research suggesting that firms form
expectations and set prices according to what is
happening in their own industry and experience, while
putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central
bank credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many
consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when
supply shocks abound.

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused
considerable upset this month, even though it is a reversion
to historical and international practice. The card schemes
used to impose “no-surcharge” rules, until the RBA banned
these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The
turnaround represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its
longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people to
choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer

made sense in a largely cashless economy. In any case, least-
cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in
the hands of the merchants who actually bear the cost.
In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker
price, leaving the total cost to the consumer unchanged, just
more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers
have absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first
place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated that around

16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though,
some firms have lifted their prices by more than any reasonable
estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway
coffee has been the centre of consumer discontent.
Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main
instigator of this month’s slump in consumer sentiment. Petrol
‘Cost-Plus’ costs
Forecast changes

• A full update to our forecasts was published in our October
Market Outlook  on Thursday 15 October.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and
the before-and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week
shows that higher interest rates also contributed to the decline.
In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the
RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses passing on increased

costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to
make that increase stick – is exactly the kind of inflationary
environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been
subjected to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from
pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel to
water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which
for decades were a reliable source of cost reductions, are
nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins

intact, demonstrates that demand is not weak enough to
prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations
might be higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this
as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair
Work Commission did hand down an above-average increase in
award wages this year and this was probably passed through
to prices in hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base

wages growth has been moderate for some years. Business
surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue
lately. This is why we were puzzled by the passage in the RBA
minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported
in business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate
consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU) has risen. It is
a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to
overshoot your forecasts, as the latest labour market data have

done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.
Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do
not have the market power, though coffee may be an exception
because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And
as noted above, large firms with everyday-low-price strategies
will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply chain.
Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on.
Some observers might see this as corporate greed, but another

way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality.
“Entrenched expectations that cost increases
can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against,
but only indirectly."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
It turns out that cost-plus pricing is especially common in
areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated
a lot in recent years.
There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The

failures of so many building firms a few years ago, squeezed
between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and
fixed-price contracts, were a case in point. Normally, there is
a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect
firms never to pass cost increases through to prices.
The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits
the imagination away from finding responses that might save

costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost
efficiency. It was equally limiting in times of soft demand: in
the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a belief
that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they
could not possibly accept cost increases, including in wages.
Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be
consistent with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle
of a central bank governor complaining that wages growth

was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to
economise on it and thereby boost productivity?
A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of
supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing as the ‘inflation
expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation,
but it is related. The stronger the tendency towards cost-plus
pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the

distinction matters. The recent academic literature includes
some fascinating empirical evidence  from New Zealand that
suggests that beliefs about current and future overall inflation
have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are
shaped less by economy-wide inflation developments than by
what is happening to their costs and in their industry.
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot

like firms (in aggregate) setting prices according to expected
inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something
quite different is going on. If firms’ beliefs about future
economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing
decisions, then neither does the credibility of a central bank’s
commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism to
get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand
enough to convince firms that cost-plus will not fly in the

market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers
or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when supply shocks
abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade
or even self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that
happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.
The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can
be reduced. That is more likely to be a question for regulators
than for central banks.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
bias towards an October or December decision. Williams
stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in
this cycle given known information. And Waller also showed
confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are

doing some of the FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest
rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating that
one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation
near the 2.0%yr medium-term target. However, as discussed
in this month’s Market Outlook , capacity pressures are likely
to see inflation consistently above target, rather than below,
in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity in the
housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to

remain modestly restrictive over the forecast horizon and will
also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade
account. At USD133.5bn, the September result was a whisker
inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More
impressively, throughout 2025 and 2026 year-to-date the
surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the
average of 2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth
of export growth is as impressive as the headline annual gain of

36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese
investment in neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time,
both trade and income receipts from offshore investment should
buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound
foreign investment should moderate the pace of gains against
other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the
evolution of producer prices across Asia, and the likelihood

of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent
decades continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was
one of the key drivers of declining goods prices. While
still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing
development to instead be driven by capital, technology and
energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking
production are also increasingly prioritising resilience, security
and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore more

probably future supply-chain developments will help contain
rising costs through productivity than drive sustained price
declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September
Labour Force Survey . Employment surprised to the
downside, –39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force
participation from August’s near-historic high largely offset.
The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August

was revised up.
Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless,
the take home point from the update is that the labour
market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs
below working-age population growth. We expect the
unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter
financial conditions and cost-of-living pressures restrict
activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast
peak is near at 4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the

communication challenge for the RBA. The September minutes
were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing
on materialising upside risks to inflation. But, with the
unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption
of 4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the
labour market is becoming harder to defend. Still, with a clear
focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our
near-term CPI forecasts in the October Market Outlook  – we

still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in
September, taking the annual rate up to 3.7%yr. Energy prices
were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation
was a touch above consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr).
Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months
(0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4% (3.1%yr) on a

0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport
services and a 0.3% increase in medical care services (2.7%yr
and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release
of the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures,
with the headline and core measures up 6.3%yr and 5.0%yr
respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these
developments, firms across multiple districts citing tariffs,
insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost

pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect
further price increases as contracts/hedges roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is
enough signal in these reports to warrant a follow-up hike
at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee
members believe they are near the end of the tightening cycle
and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman
both showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Downward revisions to 2027 outlook
This week we have updated our New Zealand economic
outlook for the next year, in particular reflecting the latest
developments in the energy market and key financial
variables (long-term interest rates and the exchange rate).

After last week’s marking down of our forecast for through-
year GDP growth in 2026 to 2.5%yr, this week we have
reduced our forecast of growth in 2027 by 0.3ppts to 2.7%yr.
This downward revision reflects a less positive assessment
of the outlooks for consumption and investment (including
residential investment) considering the more elevated path
assumed for energy prices and a higher near-term track for
long-term interest rates (as discussed in our updated Market

Outlook, published earlier this week).
A partial offset to weaker growth in domestic demand is
provided by a slightly more positive outlook for exports
(particularly exports of manufactured goods and tourism
inflows) because of a downward revision to our expectations
for the NZ dollar. We now expect NZD/USD to end this
year around current levels at 0.56 – 3c lower than expected
previously – before appreciating to around 0.64 over the
course of 2027 as interest rate differentials with the US

gradually close and as the USD appreciates. We think that the
low value of the NZ dollar – in trade-weighted terms now at a
17-year low – helps to explain the recent strong performance
in the previously moribund manufacturing sector, as well as
tourist inflows from key markets such as Australia.
Last week we raised our forecast for CPI inflation at year end
to 4.3%yr, largely on account of recent developments in global
energy prices (although the weak NZ dollar has compounded the

effect). This week we have slightly revised up our forecast for
inflation for the first half of 2027. But because energy prices are
assumed to decline in 2027 from what is now a higher starting
level, our 2027 year-end forecast for inflation has nudged
down 0.1ppts to 1.6%yr. Needless to say, forecasts of headline
inflation, in particular, are subject to significant revision given the
uncertain outlook for the Middle East conflict and uncertainty

about how that will be reflected in global energy prices.
Ahead of next week’s very important Q3 CPI report – which
could prove decisive – we have made no changes to our
forecast for the RBNZ’s policy settings. So, for now we
continue to think that despite the higher near-term inflation
outlook the RBNZ will stick with the strategy communicated
in the September Monetary Policy Statement, i.e. that at the
vv28 October meeting it will pause to assess the impact of this

year’s 50bps of policy tightening, before considering a further
upward adjustment in December.
Turning to this week’s data, the news from the housing market
remained decidedly downbeat. House sales fell a further 4% in
September – the seventh consecutive month of decline – to be
12% lower than a year earlier. With the stock of listings at an
11-year high, the median selling time remains elevated. And
so unsurprisingly, house prices fell for a fourth consecutive

month and are now down 1.6%yr. Prices remain especially
soft in the North Island, with prices down more than 3%yr in
Auckland and more than 4%yr in Wellington. Given upward
pressures on mortgage rates, broader strength in the economy
– including a stronger labour market – will likely be required to
reverse this trend. On that score, MBIE’s measure of online job
advertisements was little changed in September, continuing
the flat trend since the beginning of this year.

In other news, retail spending, as measured by electronic card
payments, decreased 0.4%m/m in September following an
unrevised 0.9% decline in August. This was a weaker result than
we had expected based on the recent sawtooth pattern and
our own internal data. We should treat these data with some
suspicion given that for quite a while now the Stats NZ series
seems not to have reflected the spending seen through banks’
own card spending networks, perhaps suggesting changes in the

payments arena may be leaving the official sector data behind.
Even so, unsurprisingly, with petrol prices rebounding late in
the month, spending on fuel increased 2.9%mth. However,
core spending, which excludes spending on fuel and vehicles,
fell 0.7%m/m. While spending on consumables (groceries)
increased modestly in September, this was at the expense of
spending on ‘nice to haves’. Spending on hospitality suffered a
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

further 2.3%mth fall following a 1.9%mth decline in August,
and spending on apparel replicated the 2.0%mth decline
reported in August. Spending on durable goods also fell
slightly in September (-0.4%mth).
Despite the weak outcome for the August and September

months, retail spending over the September quarter still
eked out a minor 0.1% gain, while core spending increased
0.4%. Given higher prices, at face value this points to a weak
quarter for volumes (next week’s CPI report will cast more
light on pricing behaviour during the quarter). It is worth
noting that total retail spending, as will be depicted in the
official quarterly retail sales survey released next month, could
point to somewhat stronger growth. Purchases of big-ticket

items that often are bought on credit – such as cars, furniture
and appliances – are underrepresented in the electronic card
spending survey.
Given subdued domestic demand, local hospitality businesses
have relied on foreign visitors to drive growth in spending.
And the good news is that visitor arrivals increased to a new
post-Covid high in August to be up 8%yr. Arrivals of Chinese
citizens have grown by almost a third to sit near pre-Covid
levels in recent months, while arrivals of US citizens grew

17%yr. However, the Middle East conflict has impacted
long-haul arrivals from Europe (UK arrivals are down 8%yr).
Meanwhile, inflows of migrants remain at modest levels,
totalling around 20,000 in the year to August – about half
what might be expected on average over the cycle.
News from the manufacturing sector remained positive in
September, with the BusinessNZ Manufacturing PMI rising
to a 3-month high of 56.5 in September. Improvement was
seen across all subcomponents of the index, with the new

orders index rising 1.8pts to 57.0. We think that the recovery in
manufacturing this year owes much to the weakness of the NZ
dollar – especially against the Australian dollar – and activity
associated with a strong domestic primary sector and an
emerging recovery in domestic residential construction activity.
Last but certainly not least, ahead of next week’s all-important
Q3 CPI, the Selected Prices report for September was firmer
than expected. That was mainly due to larger than expected

increases in airfares and holiday accommodation costs, which
offset softer than expected food prices.
Accounting for the September prices data, we’ve left our
forecast for Q3 inflation  at 0.7%. That would see the annual
inflation rate dropping back to 3.7%, down from 4.1% in the
year to June. Underlying that move has been a moderation in
fuel prices after they rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle
East war. That’s balanced against increases in council rates,

building costs and food prices.
Our forecast is lower than the RBNZ’s September MPS
forecast for a 0.8% rise (+3.9%yr). However, with some large
swings in volatile items like fuel, the RBNZ will likely focus
on measures of core inflation. Core inflation was already at
firm levels in the June quarter, with key measures sitting in
the upper part of the RBNZ’s target band. We expect that
they will take another step higher in September, to sit around

3%. Upside surprises in core inflation would be an important
development for the RBNZ ahead of the October OCR review,
as this could suggest that the lower NZD and higher oil prices
are boosting underlying price pressures by more and sooner
than they may have expected.

### Page 7

Q3 is likely to produce a very similar outcome to Q2, with 
quarterly growth of around 1.0% holding the annual rate at 
4.3%yr. Year-to-date, growth will remain around 4.7%ytd 
thanks to the Q1 result.
The detail and partial data for September will again highlight 
the weak state of the consumer and domestic-oriented 
businesses, but also the incredible strength of exports. With 
the trade surplus back at record highs, there are limits to the 
contribution possible from net exports hence, necessitating 
a strengthening of domestic demand if aggregate growth is 
to continue at the rate authorities intend into 2027. Expect 
additional stimulus through the turn of the year.

## Document 301: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

## Document 332: Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

7 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES

In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

## Document 375: NZ labour market preview, Q3 2026 (2026-11-02)

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Turning point?
• We expect the unemployment rate to edge
down to 5.5% for the September quarter.
• Job numbers have been gaining momentum
in recent months, and now look to be enough
to outstrip population growth. However, the
participation rate will be the deciding factor.
• Despite cost-of-living pressures on workers,
the existing slack in the labour market is likely
to keep wage growth subdued.
• Our forecasts are a little more positive than

what the RBNZ assumed in their September
policy statement. Assuming they deliver on
an OCR hike this afternoon, the labour market
surveys could have an important bearing on
whether they follow up with another hike in
December.

The New Zealand economy has returned to growth over
the last year, despite a number of headwinds. That’s been
accompanied by some modest growth in jobs, though
to date it hasn’t been enough to prevent a further rise

in the unemployment rate. However, we think that next
Wednesday’s labour market reports could show the jobs
market reaching a turning point.
We expect the unemployment rate to ease from 5.6%
to 5.5% in the September quarter, which would mark
the first fall in almost five years. That’s not a firmly held
view, given the usual margin of uncertainty around this
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of Q3 labour market surveys:
4 November, 10:45am.
28 Oct 2026 | Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
measure. But we’re now seeing consistent evidence
that jobs growth is outstripping the modest pace of
population growth. We also note that the previous result
was 5.56% in unrounded terms, so it wouldn’t take much
to tip the rounded rate down to 5.5% this time.
On the whole, our forecasts are a little stronger than
what the RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary
Policy Statement. While our unemployment rate forecast

is identical, we’re more positive on both employment
and labour force participation. Meanwhile, our pick of a
0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index is slightly ahead of the
RBNZ’s estimate of 0.4%.
Assuming that the RBNZ delivers the OCR hike that we
expect this afternoon, we think they will be noncommittal
about the chances of another hike at the 9 December
Monetary Policy Statement. In that respect, the labour
market surveys could be important in setting market

expectations for the December review, as there aren’t
that many major data releases between now and then.
Forecast details.
We expect the Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS)
to show a 0.5% rise in employment for the September
quarter. That’s broadly in line with the Monthly
Employment Indicator (MEI), with the September release
this morning showing a third straight month of growth
in filled jobs. We note that employment growth in the
HLFS has actually outstripped the MEI for the last nine

quarters, but it’s not clear why this has been the case and
we’re wary of extrapolating it too far into the next quarter.

Employment growth of around 0.5% would be more than
enough to absorb the modest 0.3% rise in the working-
age population, and on its own would suggest a fall in

the unemployment rate. But it also comes down to the
rate of labour force participation, which we don’t really
have much guidance on. The participation rate has been
choppy over recent quarters, and in the June quarter
we saw an unusually large 0.3pt rise to 70.7%; we’re
assuming some payback this time with a more modest
0.1pt rise to 70.8%.
We’ll also be keeping an eye on the broader indicators of
slack in the labour market, although we don’t formally use

these in our forecasts. The June quarter saw a sharp rise
in the underutilisation rate from 12.9% to 13.8%. However,
we think there’s some residual seasonality in this series,
relating to one component in particular: people who are
available for work but not actively looking. In that case,
we could see a substantial pullback in the underutilisation
measure this time.
Previously we’ve characterised the labour market as a
“low-fire, low-hire” environment, with much of the rise in

unemployment reflecting the difficulty of breaking into
the workforce, rather than job losses. There are some
signs of a thaw emerging, with job vacancies picking up
from their lows over the last year and business surveys
pointing to a lift in hiring intentions. But we still expect
that it will be a long road to recovery – our current
forecasts see the unemployment rate at 5% by the end of
next year.
While cost-of-living pressures are again rearing their

head as a major concern for workers, the existing degree
of slack in the labour market will continue to make it
difficult for them to push for bigger pay increases. We
expect a 0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index (LCI) for
the September quarter, which would nudge the annual
growth rate up slightly to 2.1%. That said, we don’t have a
lot to go on for this forecast. The MEI includes a measure
of median weekly earnings, which has shown no sign of
acceleration so far – but this is only a very loose indicator

of wage pressures, as it will also reflect any changes in
the composition of jobs and pay rates.
